## 6. A paved client

- **The app object.** `snapback4/app` exports `createApp({ url, device?,
  sessionStore? })`. It returns `{ client, auth }`, where `auth.state` is
  `restoring`, `signed-out` or `signed-in`, and `auth` has `signup`,
  `login`, `logout` and `guest`. It persists the session and reopens the
  local outbox. Pending writes keep their ids across restarts without
  authored intent storage. In React, `<AppProvider app>` and `useAuth()`
  compose with the existing hooks. Client opening is provisional; the prior
  owner remains live until success. Replacement journals retirement, then
  commits the bearer and epoch in one atomic store write under the session
  lock. Automatic rotation and outbox replay start after that commit.
  Restoration and rotation retain their epoch; replacement of a live bearer
  advances it. Logout atomically writes a noncredential tombstone carrying
  the epoch and retirement obligations. In the browser, IndexedDB is the
  authoritative session state (2026-09-25 ruling): every session replacement or
  logout awaits its strict transaction commit inside the session Web Lock.
  localStorage mirrors that value and synchronously captures mutation intent;
  it is not authority for a locked admission or recovery check. Chromium's
  renderer caches can remain stale across Web Lock holders. A stale mirror may
  capture a click, but admission checks IndexedDB under the lock and returns
  `E_AUTH` instead of admitting or sending it after logout. Legacy mirror-only
  sessions require sign-in, and unadmitted records without the new authority
  stamp cannot be promoted; already committed outbox custody remains intact.
  Before opening a scope, the session lock protects migration of the maximum
  legacy mirror and IndexedDB epoch into IndexedDB and the epoch mirror. This
  imports an epoch fence, never credentials. Authoritative reads repair the
  session mirror, including deleting it for absent authority. A locked admission
  refusal schedules the app's authorization check after releasing the lock, so
  a confirmed stale scope also loses its observations. Every browser journaled
  write retains its original store binding until it leaves the outbox, including
  fresh clicks captured while startup validation is in flight; adoption of a
  replacement backend retires them without dispatch.
  **Busy-group authority reads (BG, 2026-10-02).** Each ordinary app authority
  check reads the legacy epoch marker and the session record in one fresh
  IndexedDB read transaction under that same session lock. It awaits transaction
  completion, repairs both mirrors best-effort, observes the maximum epoch and
  checks the same principal/tombstone fences before releasing the lock. This
  replaces three database opens/transactions (including a duplicate session
  read), without sharing results between checks or trusting mirror credentials.
  Opening/migration, strict writes and journal admission retain their existing
  procedures. The public residency before/after checks require two locked read
  transactions rather than six; the peer-logout, stale-mirror and failed-commit
  browser tests still govern the fence. See the
  [BG qualification](../snapback4/reports/20261001-bg-busy-group-render/REPORT.md).
  **BG round 1:** on an open IndexedDB-authority scope, overlapping facade/transport
  calls queued before its read starts may share that check's linearization point. The pending batch is
  cleared before lock acquisition; any call arriving during or after the read
  queues a new fresh transaction. No completed verdict is reused. Transaction
  completion, mirror repair, immediate epoch fencing, strict session writes and
  journal admission keep their existing requirements. This removes duplicate
  concurrent reads without extending authority past a later logout. Memory/native
  stores and preparing scopes retain their original per-call awaited persistence
  scheduling: a later held journal admission cannot overtake the first request's
  authority check.
  All browser handoffs toward delivery share this binding check, including
  orphan-tab adoption. Unknown destination identity defers a bound orphan in
  its source store; mismatch retires it before enqueueing. Ordinary and
  keepalive requests carry the original `store_id`, so server validation before
  execution and deduplication also fences replacement after client validation.
  A server `E_STORE_REPLACED` retires the write with that named refusal.
  An overlapping ordinary request owns settlement for its id, so a keepalive
  refusal cannot override its committed success. A keepalive refusal without
  that owner rechecks terminal history and outbox custody in the serialized
  device transaction, then commits the receipt and retirement atomically.
  An aborted receipt transaction retains custody and publishes no refusal.
  A known local terminal outcome, sent or failed, is never replaced. If its
  receipt save aborts, persist it before sync, replay or replacement handling
  touches that id. A retry with a known receipt settles it locally with the
  original sequence/refusal instead of issuing another POST.
  The exception is an unknown-outcome `E_STORE_REPLACED`: a success-qualified
  `sent_seq` or `observed_seq` proves success. Discard its unpersisted refusal
  before saving, and overwrite an existing replacement-refusal receipt only
  when the device transaction verifies the matching qualified outbox sequence.
  Stream acknowledgement lookup includes only `_ops` rows whose stored outcome
  is `sent`; the additive `sent` map carries that qualification as id → sequence.
  The device retains the qualifier with the outbox binding and exposes it only
  when that sequence is published. Legacy sequence-only observations, including
  ones previously promoted to `sent_seq`, prove a decision without its outcome.
  With no known terminal receipt, they remain pending and retain custody until a
  same-id POST to the original store returns its recorded outcome. This replay
  may correct an unknown replacement refusal to sent or failed; the persistence
  transaction verifies the original binding before replacing stored history.
  On both web transports, the retained receipt binding takes precedence over
  the journal binding and the device's current identity. Adoption shares a
  durably verified correction over an existing `E_STORE_REPLACED` in place
  before deleting its source, preserving receipt retention order.
  A caller's lookup of an id this device holds no outbox entry for applies the
  same rule: shared history recovers an absent receipt or a verified outcome
  answers over the reader's own `E_STORE_REPLACED`; every other local outcome
  answers first. Lookup rechecks custody after the shared read and recomputes
  through local custody if adoption added an entry. Settlement reads strictly
  this device's own history, even when the id is absent. The opener reads shared
  ids only to remember used mutation ids and route retries through lookup;
  it never copies or settles the shared outcomes.
  Neither qualified evidence nor replay replaces authored refusals such as
  `NOT_ADMIN`, or refreshes receipt retention. Native devices advertise
  `receipt_outcomes`; a JavaScript upgrade refuses an older module until rebuilt.
  Delivery receipts and outcome-qualified outbox sequences establish terminal custody;
  a local admission-storage error with an unknown outcome does not, and must
  leave the captured intent recoverable.
  Epoch migration
  writes only when the legacy mirror advances authoritative state; ordinary
  restoration needs no separate epoch allocation, and repairs mirrors
  best-effort even when localStorage is full.
  Every revocation path compares the
  currently stored bearer; a failed operation cannot revoke it or advance its
  epoch. Observing a newer epoch fences every older scope, including rollback
  candidates, before publication. A known server refusal conditionally
  tombstones the still-stored bearer or its known consumed predecessor after a
  failed rotation save, so later offline opens cannot reopen its replica.
  Auth state, viewer and client agree at auth and client callbacks. Hiding an
  account permanently fences exposed facades and observations independently of
  private raw retry custody; rollback exposes a new facade rather than reviving
  a previously hidden handle. A facade fence refuses new dispatches without
  replacing the outcomes of already-dispatched mutations, actions, uploads or
  refreshes. Close completes after local cleanup without waiting for a held network
  response, including unread session rotations. Already-started session persistence
  finishes locally; a late unkept successor is retired. Each mutation/action
  invocation whose response is received before public app close completes retains
  its full known outcome, including during session cleanup; otherwise the caller receives pending with its id. Requests
  are never aborted; later bodies are consumed and discarded. Durable mutation
  intent settles on reopen through the server's idempotency record. Within a runtime, mutation results, receipt lookup and
  notifications agree on each known terminal outcome, even when receipt saves
  keep failing. Online custody retains an unpersisted receipt summary in memory
  and notifies once; lookup retries persistence without reporting pending. The
  original online durable intent records its dispatch store before dispatch.

//! Explicit, preview-first schema adaptation. Application screens stay owned.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#14-wave-14-persona-to-production — apply authorizes only the displayed seat grants.
use crate::project::Project;
use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType, RuleExpr};
use snapback4_lang::{ast::{Effect, TableDecl}, lexer::{self, Kind}};
use std::{collections::BTreeSet, path::{Path, PathBuf}};

struct Change { name: String, after: String }
struct Plan { sources: Vec<(String, String)>, changes: Vec<Change>, messages: Vec<String> }

pub(super) fn adapt(root: &Path, apply: bool) -> i32 {
    let project = Project { root: root.to_owned() };
    let plan = match plan(&project) { Ok(plan) => plan, Err(error) => { eprintln!("E_INPUT: {error}"); return 2; } };
    println!("Production adaptation{}: application screens stay unchanged", if apply { " (apply)" } else { " (preview; no files written)" });
    for message in &plan.messages { println!("  {message}"); }
    for change in &plan.changes {
        println!("  replace snapback/{} with:\n{}", change.name, change.after);
    }
    if !apply {
        println!("Review the seat grants, then run: snapback4 init --adapt --production --apply");
        return 0;
    }
    // Check the whole plan before its first write. Do not follow a source symlink.
    if let Err(error) = preflight(&project, &plan.sources) {
        eprintln!("E_INPUT: {error}"); return 2;
    }
    for change in &plan.changes {
        let path = project.schema_dir().join(&change.name);
        if let Err(error) = super::replace_file(&path, &change.after) {
            eprintln!("E_INPUT: {}: {error}; some earlier displayed files may have been written", path.display()); return 2;
        }
    }
    println!("Applied {} schema file(s). Run snapback4 check; map the listed dev principals before deploying. Signup grants no role.", plan.changes.len());
    0
}

fn preflight(project: &Project, sources: &Vec<(String, String)>) -> Result<(), String> {
    if project.sources().ok().as_ref() != Some(sources) { return Err("source set changed during production adaptation; preview again".into()); }
    for (name, before) in sources {
        let path = project.schema_dir().join(name);
        if std::fs::symlink_metadata(&path).is_ok_and(|m| m.file_type().is_symlink())
            || std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap_or_default() != *before {
            return Err(format!("{} changed during production adaptation or is a symlink; preview again", path.display()));
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn plan(project: &Project) -> Result<Plan, String> {
    let sources = project.sources().map_err(|e| format!("snapback/*.q: {e}"))?;
    if sources.is_empty() { return Err("production adaptation needs snapback/*.q".into()); }
    let initial = snapback4_lang::compile(&sources);
    if !initial.diagnostics.is_empty() { return Err(format!("repair the schema before production adaptation: {:?}", initial.diagnostics)); }
    let mut revised = sources.clone();
    let mut messages = vec!["GET /auth/config reports development; guest sessions POST /auth/guest before reads. Bare use identity keeps guests; use identity password removes them.".into()];
    let has_identity = sources.iter().any(|(name, source)| snapback4_lang::parse_document(name, source).0.identity.is_some());
    if !has_identity {
        revised[0].1.insert_str(0, "use identity\n");
        messages.push(format!("add use identity to snapback/{} (password accounts and guests)", revised[0].0));
    } else { messages.push("keep the existing identity methods".into()); }
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&revised);
    let blocked = super::super::readiness::blocked_tables(&bound);
    if blocked.is_empty() { messages.push("no N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER tables".into()); }
    else { messages.push(format!("N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER tables (including blocked dependents): {}", blocked.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))); }
    let mut names: BTreeSet<_> = bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.name.clone()).collect();
    let module = Path::new(&revised[0].0).file_stem().unwrap().to_string_lossy();
    let prefix = if module == "schema" { String::new() } else { format!("{module}.") };
    let mut mutations = String::new();
    // A roster has a principal in a unique identity. Other roots need explicit
    // application policy; report them without inventing a catalogue or profile.
    for name in &blocked {
        let table = bound.schema.table(name).unwrap();
        let roster = table.columns.iter().any(|(col, ty)| matches!(ty, ColumnType::Principal)
            && table.indexes.values().any(|index| index.unique && index.columns().contains(col)));
        if !roster || table.through.is_some() {
            messages.push(format!("{name}: review an application-specific first writer (no roster seat grant generated)")); continue;
        }
        let base = format!("bootstrap{name}");
        let mut operation = base.clone(); let mut suffix = 2;
        while !names.insert(format!("{prefix}{operation}")) { operation = format!("{base}{suffix}"); suffix += 1; }
        let inputs: Vec<_> = table.columns.iter().filter(|(column, kind)| column.as_str() != "id"
            && !table.maintained.contains_key(*column)
            && (!table.defaults.contains_key(*column) || matches!(kind, ColumnType::Principal) || column.as_str() == "role")).collect();
        let args = inputs.iter().map(|(col, ty)| format!("seat_{col}: {ty}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let fields = inputs.iter().map(|(col, _)| format!("{col}: seat_{col}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        mutations.push_str(&format!("\nmutation {operation}({args}):\n  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR\n  return insert {name} {{ {fields} }}\n"));
        messages.push(format!("{name}: add {operation}; allow service:ops to read and insert this roster. Existing account grants stay in place; signup grants no role."));
        for (file, source) in &mut revised {
            let (document, _) = snapback4_lang::parse_document(file, source);
            if let Some(decl) = document.tables.iter().find(|decl| decl.name == *name) {
                *source = seat_rules(file, source, decl, &table.rules.read, &table.rules.insert)?;
                break;
            }
        }
    }
    revised[0].1.push_str(&mutations);
    let checked = snapback4_lang::compile(&revised);
    if !checked.diagnostics.is_empty() { return Err(format!("the proposed roster policy does not compile; no files written: {:?}", checked.diagnostics)); }
    messages.push("Mint a private seat with snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment; call each bootstrap mutation with real account principals. Never seed a deployed store.".into());
    frontend_principals(&project.root, &mut messages)?;
    let changes = sources.iter().cloned().zip(revised).filter_map(|((name, before), (_, after))| (before != after).then_some(Change { name, after })).collect();
    Ok(Plan { sources, changes, messages })
}

fn allows_seat(rule: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
    use snapback4_core::{schema::RuleTerm, Value};
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(value) | Value::String(value)))
        | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(value) | Value::String(value)), RuleTerm::Viewer) => value == "service:ops",
        RuleExpr::Or(rules) => rules.iter().any(allows_seat),
        _ => false,
    }
}

fn seat_rules(file: &str, source: &str, table: &TableDecl, read: &RuleExpr, insert: &RuleExpr) -> Result<String, String> {
    let mut lines: Vec<String> = source.lines().map(|line| format!("{line}\n")).collect();
    let mut edits = Vec::new();
    let (tokens, _) = lexer::lex(file, source);
    for (effect, rule) in [(Effect::Read, read), (Effect::Insert, insert)] {
        let grant = "viewer = 'service:ops'";
        if allows_seat(rule) { continue; }
        if let Some(decl) = table.rules.iter().find(|decl| decl.effect == effect) {
            let start = decl.site.line as usize - 1;
            let token = tokens.iter().position(|token| token.site.line == decl.site.line && token.value == effect.word()).ok_or("could not locate roster rule")?;
            let end = tokens[token..].iter().find(|token| matches!(token.kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Eof)).ok_or("could not locate roster rule end")?.site.line as usize;
            let old = lines[start..end.min(lines.len())].concat();
            let (left, value) = old.split_once("<-").ok_or("roster rule needs <-")?;
            // Group the original expression, preserving bindings, comments and
            // every existing branch. The closing line cannot be commented out.
            let new = format!("{}<- (\n{}\n  ) or {grant}\n", left, value.trim_end());
            edits.push((start, end.min(lines.len()), new));
        } else if effect == Effect::Read && table.public.is_some() {
            // Public rows already admit the seat; preserve the authored reason.
        } else {
            edits.push((table.site.line as usize, table.site.line as usize, format!("  {} <- {grant}\n", effect.word())));
        }
    }
    edits.sort_by_key(|(start, _, _)| std::cmp::Reverse(*start));
    for (start, end, text) in edits { lines.splice(start..end, [text]); }
    Ok(lines.concat())
}

fn frontend_principals(root: &Path, messages: &mut Vec<String>) -> Result<(), String> {
    fn walk(root: &Path, dir: &Path, messages: &mut Vec<String>, depth: u8) -> Result<(), String> {
        if depth == 0 { return Ok(()); }
        let entries = std::fs::read_dir(dir).map_err(|e| format!("{}: {e}", dir.display()))?;
        let mut paths: Vec<PathBuf> = entries.map(|entry| entry.map(|e| e.path())).collect::<Result<_, _>>().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        paths.sort();
        for path in paths {
            let meta = std::fs::symlink_metadata(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if meta.file_type().is_symlink() { continue; }
            if meta.is_dir() {
                if !matches!(path.file_name().and_then(|name| name.to_str()), Some("node_modules" | ".git" | "snapback" | ".snapback4" | "target" | "dist" | "build" | ".expo" | "fixtures" | "versions" | "recipes")) {
                    walk(root, &path, messages, depth - 1)?;
                }
            } else if path.extension().and_then(|ext| ext.to_str()).is_some_and(|ext| matches!(ext, "ts" | "tsx" | "js" | "jsx" | "mjs" | "cjs" | "html")) {
                let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
                for (line, _) in text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, text)| text.contains("dev:")) {
                    messages.push(format!("{}:{}: hard-coded dev: principal; map to an authenticated account or authorized principal picker", path.strip_prefix(root).unwrap_or(&path).display(), line + 1));
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
    walk(root, root, messages, 20)
}


#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn preflight_covers_unchanged_modules_and_added_sources() {
        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-prod-preflight-{}", std::process::id()));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(root.join("snapback")).unwrap();
        let project = Project { root: root.clone() };
        let file = root.join("snapback/schema.q");
        std::fs::write(&file, "query who():\n  return viewer\n").unwrap();
        let snapshot = project.sources().unwrap();
        assert!(preflight(&project, &snapshot).is_ok());
        std::fs::write(&file, "query who():\n  return 1\n").unwrap();
        assert!(preflight(&project, &snapshot).is_err());
        std::fs::write(&file, &snapshot[0].1).unwrap();
        std::fs::write(root.join("snapback/new.q"), "query value():\n  return 2\n").unwrap();
        assert!(preflight(&project, &snapshot).is_err());
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
    }
}

# Production: a persona demo becomes an account app

Bare `use identity` keeps guests; guests call `/auth/guest` before any read;
`use identity password` removes them. `/auth/config` is GET; production needs
persisted sessions and trusted first writers because signup grants no role.

```quarry
use identity password
table labels:
  slug: text 1..40
  unique slug
  cap 100
  immutable slug
  public 'labels are visible to signed-in accounts'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
  online only
mutation provisionLabel(slug: text 1..40):
  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
  return insert labels {slug}
```
This is the minimal production bar. Profiles and teams below are optional application policy. Prove writes, outsiders and durable restart. Mint `token --service ops --release ./deployment` while serve runs, then call `/m/provisionLabel` with its bearer. No production seed door is needed. Add a root's service read branch when its writer probes existing rows. Every mutation still passes table rules (`guide deploy`).

A persona demo becomes a password-account app with self-written profiles,
a private staff directory and trusted membership enrollment. This policy is
explicit because directory privacy and enrollment authority are application decisions.

```tsx
import { useState } from "react";
import { createApp } from "snapback4/app";
import { webDevice } from "snapback4/web";
import { localStorageSessionStore } from "snapback4/auth";
import { AppProvider, useAuth, useMutation, sentResult } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
const app=createApp({url:"http://127.0.0.1:4400",device:webDevice(),sessionStore:localStorageSessionStore()});
export function ProductionApp(){return <AppProvider app={app}><Gate /></AppProvider>;}
function Gate() {
  const auth=useAuth();
  if(auth.state==="restoring")return <p>Restoring…</p>;
  if(auth.state==="signed-out")return <PasswordForm />;
  return <AccountScreen />;
}
function PasswordForm(){
  const auth=useAuth(),[email,setEmail]=useState(""),[password,setPassword]=useState(""),[why,setWhy]=useState("");
  async function submit(signup:boolean){const result=await(signup?auth.signup(email,password):auth.login(email,password));if(!result.ok)setWhy(result.why.message);}
  return <form onSubmit={e=>{e.preventDefault();void submit(false);}}>
    <input aria-label="Email" type="email" required value={email} onChange={e=>setEmail(e.target.value)}/>
    <input aria-label="Password" type="password" required minLength={8} value={password} onChange={e=>setPassword(e.target.value)}/>
    <button>Sign in</button><button type="button" onClick={()=>void submit(true)}>Sign up</button>
    {why&&<p role="alert">{why}</p>}</form>;
}
function AccountScreen(){
  const auth=useAuth(),save=useMutation(api.register),[name,setName]=useState("");
  async function submit(){const displayName=name.trim();if(!displayName||displayName.length>80)return;const write=await save.run({displayName});if(sentResult(write)!==undefined)setName(current=>current.trim()===displayName?"":current);}
  return <><p>Account {auth.viewer}</p><input aria-label="Display name" value={name} onChange={e=>setName(e.target.value)}/>
    <button disabled={save.inFlight} onClick={()=>void submit()}>Save profile</button>
    {save.last?.state==="failed"&&<p role="alert">{save.last.why.message}</p>}
    <button onClick={()=>void auth.logout()}>Sign out</button></>;
}
```

## Production transition checklist

1. Preview `init --adapt --production`, then review and use `--apply`. It adds
   bare `use identity` only when no identity is declared, lists blocked tables,
   emits one typed service-seat provisioning mutation per roster and shows its
   explicit read/insert seat grants. It names frontend `dev:` references without
   editing screens. Non-roster roots still need application policy.
   Run `check`, then `why N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER` in the project. It lists
   **all** fixture-only first writers and their blocked dependents. Its rewrite
   proposes a seat mutation for each root, and one per declared `role` enum
   value. Review these proposals: add `viewer = 'service:ops'` to the table's
   insert rule, authorize required probes and run `check` again. The CLI never
   silently grants a seat or changes the schema. Separate roster tables get
   separate proposals; other application role encodings need explicit policy.
2. Map every `dev:NAME` owner, fixture role and relationship to a real account
   principal. Signup grants no role. Provision the initial roster with the
   seat; `grantStaff` below is the concrete first-role example.
3. Persist sessions with `localStorageSessionStore()` in browsers and
   `secureStoreSessionStore(SecureStore)` from `snapback4-expo` on native.
   Device-less identity apps require an explicit `sessionStore` in TypeScript.
4. Keep the password form available alongside the development persona picker.
   Fetch `/auth/config`, and render personas only when `development === true`.
   `serve` reports false and refuses personas. Do not derive a role from a
   selector, query string or principal prefix: read the application's roster.
5. Use the authorized directory and shared-principal controls below, then run
   the account/outsider/restart acceptance at the end of this card. See
   the full auth-form and proof-kit recipes in `guide client`/`guide testing`.

## Password UI and auth transitions

Replace `?as=alice`, `usePersonaApp` and fixed `dev:` owners with `createApp`
using a persisted session store (`guide client`, password React/Expo). Choose
`use identity password` when guests must be removed, and retain `use personas` only for
development tests. Production principals come from signup/login, not names.
`init --adapt --production` previews the identity and first-writer schema plan;
`--apply` writes it and leaves application screens unchanged. Map each listed
`dev:` location to the authenticated viewer or an authorized picker. Directory
privacy and who may invite remain explicit application policy.

`AppProvider` remounts children on every auth-state transition. A draft inside
PasswordForm is intentionally reset by signup/login/logout; store a mode choice
or unsent work above the provider if it must survive. `useSignedInViewer()` is
for a signed-in AccountScreen; `restoring` is not a signed-in viewer. The browser setup above persists sessions; on Expo replace webDevice with
nativeDevice and use SecureStore (`guide client`). Never keep credentials in an account directory.

## Password plus development personas

Keep the account form as the default. This outer selector reads the server's
cached development configuration when interpreting `?as=` and refreshes it in the
background. A missing cache defaults to account sign-in. The SDK restores its
last session and held data without waiting for the server. Cache failure or a
failed refresh never replaces the account screen. The cache is discovery UI,
not authority: serve still refuses every persona. On a first visit, wait for
the development links before choosing a persona. Its persona list is the application's
declared development list; it grants no production role. Mount `AccountEntry`
above `AppProvider`; the `children` contain Gate/PasswordForm/AccountScreen from
above. An account screen reads `myStaff` below to show roster roles and mounts
`SharePrincipal` for a signed-in account.

```tsx
import {useEffect, useState, type ReactNode} from "react";
import {createApp, type App} from "snapback4/app";
import {webDevice} from "snapback4/web";
import {localStorageSessionStore} from "snapback4/auth";
import {AppProvider} from "snapback4/react";
export function AccountEntry({url, children}: {url: string; children: ReactNode}) {
  const [ready, setReady] = useState<{app: App; development: boolean} | null>(null);
  useEffect(() => {
    const key = `snapback4:auth-config:${url}`;
    let development = false;
    try { development = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(key) ?? "null")?.development === true; } catch {}
    const requested = new URLSearchParams(location.search).get("as");
    const persona = development && ["alice", "bob"].includes(requested ?? "") ? requested! : undefined;
    const owned = createApp({url, device: webDevice(), sessionStore: localStorageSessionStore(), ...(persona ? {persona} : {})});
    setReady({app: owned, development});
    const controller = new AbortController();
    void fetch(`${url}/auth/config`, {method: "GET", signal: controller.signal})
      .then(async response => {
        if (!response.ok) return;
        const config = await response.json();
        if (controller.signal.aborted) return;
        const development = config.development === true;
        try { localStorage.setItem(key, JSON.stringify({development})); } catch {}
        setReady({app: owned, development});
      }).catch(() => {});
    return () => { controller.abort(); void owned.close(); };
  }, [url]);
  if (!ready) return <p>Loading sign-in…</p>;
  return <>{ready.development && <nav aria-label="Development identity">
    <a href="?">Password account</a> <a href="?as=alice">Alice</a> <a href="?as=bob">Bob</a>
  </nav>}<AppProvider app={ready.app}>{children}</AppProvider></>;
}
```

## Profiles, private pickers, roles and linked memberships

This whole schema compiles. Each account writes its own display profile.
An operator enrolls staff and members. A staff directory exposes display names
and principals only to staff, not every account; a picker for a team narrows to
that team's members. Do not offer an unrestricted principal picker. Profiles
and membership creation are linked in `register`/`enroll`; enrollment first
requires a real profile, so opaque ids are not silently invented.

```quarry
use identity password

table staff:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists staff[viewer]
  sync
table profiles:
  principal: principal
  displayName: text 1..80
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or exists staff[viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- .principal = viewer
  update <- .principal = viewer
  online only
table catalogues:
  slug: text 1..80
  label: text 1..80
  unique slug
  cap 100
  immutable slug
  public 'catalogue labels are visible to signed-in accounts'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
  update <- viewer = 'service:ops'
  online only
table teams:
  name: text 1..80
  read <- exists members[.id, viewer] or exists staff[viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- exists staff[viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops'
  online only
table members:
  team: teams
  principal: principal
  unique team, principal
  immutable team, principal
  read <- exists members[.team, viewer] or exists staff[viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- exists staff[viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops'
  online only
mutation register(displayName: text 1..80):
  return upsert profiles[principal = viewer] { displayName }
mutation grantStaff(principal: principal):
  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
  p = profiles[principal = principal] ! else NO_PROFILE
  return insert staff { principal: p.principal }
mutation provisionCatalogue(slug: text 1..80, label: text 1..80):
  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
  return upsert catalogues[slug = slug] { label }
mutation createTeam(name: text 1..80):
  require exists staff[viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_STAFF
  return insert teams { name }
mutation enroll(team: teams, principal: principal):
  require exists staff[viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_STAFF
  t = teams[team] ! else NOT_FOUND
  p = profiles[principal = principal] ! else NO_PROFILE
  return insert members { team: t.id, principal: p.principal }
query directory(c: cursor ?):
  require exists staff[viewer] else NOT_STAFF
  return profiles first 20 by .id after c { principal, displayName }
query teammates(team: teams, c: cursor ?):
  require exists members[team, viewer] or exists staff[viewer] else NOT_MEMBER
  return members where .team = team first 20 by .id after c { principal }
query catalogue(c: cursor ?):
  return catalogues first 20 by .id after c
query myProfile():
  return profiles[principal = viewer]
query myStaff():
  return staff[principal = viewer]
```

A staff picker uses the authorized directory; a failed read produces a refusal,
not an unrestricted fallback list. Mount this inside the signed-in screen:

```tsx
import { readPresentation, usePage } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function StaffPicker({onPick}:{onPick:(principal:string)=>void}) {
  const page=usePage(api.directory,{}),status=readPresentation(page.read);
  return <>{status.why&&<p role="alert">{status.why.message}</p>}
    {status.incomplete&&<p>More accounts may exist</p>}
    <select aria-label="Account" defaultValue="" onChange={e=>onPick(e.target.value)}>
      <option value="" disabled>Choose an account</option>
      {page.items.map(p=><option key={p.principal} value={p.principal}>{p.displayName}</option>)}
    </select><button disabled={!page.hasMore} onClick={page.loadMore}>More</button></>;
}
```

The teammate query above lists only principals; resolve names through a separately
authorized profile read. This policy does not make all teammates' profiles
readable. Either let staff choose by the directory, or materialise a minimal
team directory with the team's read rule. Keep emails and private profile fields
in a separate self-readable table. For a group-synced app, staff's independent
cross-group grant cannot form a second group audience: enroll staff in each
team roster or stay online. `guide rule-recipes` covers copied audiences and
one-group limits. Wait for scope acquisition before treating an offline
membership or page as complete.

## Invitation UI by shared principal

Replace a persona-name picker with two signed-in controls: each account shares its own opaque principal, and authorized staff paste it into `enroll`. Sharing an id grants no authority; the server checks staff, team and profile. This recipe directly enrolls the invited account, without an acceptance step. For accept/revoke invitations into hidden groups, see `guide rule-recipes`.

```tsx
import { useState } from "react";
import { useAuth, useMutation } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function SharePrincipal() {
  const auth = useAuth();
  if (auth.state !== "signed-in") return null;
  return <label>Share your account id<input readOnly value={auth.viewer} onFocus={e => e.currentTarget.select()} /></label>;
}
export function InviteAccount({team}: {team: string}) {
  const invite = useMutation(api.enroll), [principal, setPrincipal] = useState("");
  async function submit() {
    const sent = principal.trim(); if (!sent) return;
    const result = await invite.run({team, principal: sent});
    if (result.state === "sent") setPrincipal(current => current.trim() === sent ? "" : current);
  }
  return <form onSubmit={e => {e.preventDefault(); void submit();}}>
    <input aria-label="Invited account id" required value={principal} onChange={e => setPrincipal(e.target.value)} />
    <button disabled={invite.inFlight}>Invite account</button>
    {invite.last?.state === "failed" && <p role="alert">{invite.last.why.message}</p>}
  </form>;
}
```
Mount InviteAccount only behind a role read, `myStaff` above. `absent` on a list can be an authorised empty result; it does not prove staff membership. Handle NOT_STAFF/NO_PROFILE even when the button was enabled. Sign up and register before sharing a principal. Seeded persona names never become production ids.

## New-app bootstrap for catalogues and first seats

Write the schema above first, then run `init --web` (or `--expo`); identity
is read from the schema. Apps with no sync use `init --web --online`.
Declare `use identity`, `use identity password`, or `use identity guests` once
for the whole project; repeating it is refused rather than replacing the active
methods.
An existing persona app runs `init --adapt --web` after adding password identity.
Signup is not staff enrollment. The first
account signs up and calls `register`, then the local operator:

```sh
npx snapback4 check
npx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
npx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
npx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment
# Keep the token private; send it as Authorization: Bearer <token>.
# POST /m/grantStaff {"args":{"principal":"user:REAL_SIGNUP_ID"}}
# POST /m/provisionCatalogue {"args":{"slug":"standard","label":"Standard"}}
# POST /m/createTeam {"args":{"name":"Production"}}
# POST /m/enroll {"args":{"team":"RETURNED_TEAM_ID","principal":"user:REAL_SIGNUP_ID"}}
```

Never seed a deployed store. The compiled `provisionCatalogue` path writes catalogue labels through the seat;
call it after deploy. Add application-specific catalogue fields deliberately. Every write, including seat writes,
passes each table's own insert/update rule. A seat's `exists` and lookups obey
read rules; give it an explicit read branch on what it must probe. The synced
staff roster permits a whole service insert branch, without adding a service
device audience (`guide deploy`, first admin).

## Real-account and rollback acceptance

Remove guest-only smoke helpers (`client.guest()` and `/auth/guest`); password
identity deliberately refuses them. Adapt proofs to signup/login; keep persona
fixtures only in development tests. On a fresh durable deployment:

1. Signup two accounts through `/auth/signup`; keep each returned principal and
   token separately. Register each profile. Restart serve and login again;
   persisted sessions and profiles survive.
2. Before enrollment, the account's `directory` refuses NOT_STAFF. The seat
   grants only the intended staff account, creates a team and enrolls both.
3. Staff sees both names; an ordinary member cannot enumerate the directory;
   an outsider cannot read teammates. A forged enrollment fails. Confirm
   account writes and sign-out, then revocation (`guide services`).
4. Save v1 source, deploy v2 with an optional profile field, run account writes,
   and `rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check`. Roll back and assert v1
   myProfile still returns displayName with the same bearer. A v2-only
   operation must answer E_OP; incompatible row writes must refuse. Forward
   with `rollback --to latest` and assert v2 values restore.

The tested schema above covers enrollment, directory privacy and linked rows.
Run this HTTP acceptance against your actual app's fields as well; storage
rollback does not restore safeguards absent from old business logic. Detailed
proof process helpers are `guide testing`; release narration is `guide changes`.


## Runnable React and Expo production slice

Every small React and Expo starter carries `recipes/production/`: a complete
schema, screen, client and acceptance cases. It runs beside the default app
without making your existing schema adopt a directory policy. In a disposable
React starter (`init --web`), run these commands as written:

```sh
cp recipes/production/schema.q snapback/schema.q
cp recipes/production/App.tsx src/App.tsx
cp recipes/production/client.ts src/client.ts
npx snapback4 check
npx snapback4 test --fixture recipes/production/fixtures
npm run typecheck
npm run build
```

For an Expo starter (`init --expo`), use this mapping instead:

```sh
cp recipes/production/schema.q snapback/schema.q
cp recipes/production/App.tsx App.tsx
cp recipes/production/client.ts src/client.ts
npx snapback4 check
npx snapback4 test --fixture recipes/production/fixtures
npm run typecheck
npm run build:web
```

Then run the starter's backend and frontend commands. Password sign-in is
always offered; development personas appear only after GET /auth/config says
development=true. Create a team to become its admin, share an account's
principal, and invite it as a member. Roles come from the roster. The principal
picker lists only that team's authorized roster. This recipe uses direct
enrollment, without an invitation acceptance step. An outsider's roster read
shows “not available”. Complete empty lists show their empty state. Drafts,
team selection and principal selection sit above release-sensitive reads;
release gates and rollback preserve them. Account changes intentionally cross
the AppProvider principal boundary. Exact's corresponding recipe is maintained
in Exact2 (EX9); these source files target React and Expo.

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! Proposals only: the application owns role policy and must add the seat rule.
use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, ColumnType};

// @ref LLP 3000.013#6.10 — role proposals never grant authority or edit source.
pub(super) fn proposal(schema: &Schema, name: &str) -> String {
    let Some(table) = schema.table(name) else { return String::new(); };
    let roles = match table.columns.get("role") {
        Some(ColumnType::Enum { variants }) => variants.iter().map(Some).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        _ => vec![None],
    };
    let mut out = "Review this proposal, add the explicit service:ops insert branch to the table, and run check. No role is granted by signup.\n```quarry\n".to_owned();
    for (index, role) in roles.into_iter().enumerate() {
        let inputs: Vec<_> = table.columns.iter().filter(|(column, kind)| column.as_str() != "id" && !(column.as_str() == "role" && role.is_some()) && (!table.defaults.contains_key(*column) || matches!(kind, ColumnType::Principal))).collect();
        let args = inputs.iter().map(|(column, kind)| format!("{column}: {kind}")).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let mut fields = inputs.iter().map(|(column, _)| (*column).clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if let Some(role) = role { fields.push(format!("role: '{}'", role.replace('\\', "\\\\").replace('\'', "\\'"))); }
        let suffix = if role.is_some() { format!("Role{}", index + 1) } else { String::new() };
        out.push_str(&format!("mutation bootstrap{name}{suffix}({}):\n  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR\n  return insert {name} {{ {} }}\n", args.join(", "), fields.join(", ")));
    }
    out.push_str("```"); out
}

#[test]
fn each_declared_role_gets_a_compilable_seat_mutation() {
    let source = "use identity password\ntable roster:\n  principal: principal default viewer\n  role: enum('admin', 'reviewer')\n  unique principal\n  read <- viewer = 'service:ops'\n  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'\n  online only\n";
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    let proposed = proposal(&bound.schema, "roster");
    assert!(proposed.contains("principal: principal"));
    assert!(proposed.contains("role: 'admin'") && proposed.contains("role: 'reviewer'"));
    let quarry = proposed.split("```quarry\n").nth(1).unwrap().split("```").next().unwrap();
    let revised = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!("{source}{quarry}"))]);
    assert!(revised.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", revised.diagnostics);
}

# Client: answers stay on screen

**Start here:** `useQuery`/`usePage`, `matchRead`/`readPresentation`; composers/receipts above rows. `useFollowing` (`guide feeds`).

```tsx
import { matchRead, readPresentation, usePage } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function Inbox() { const page = usePage(api.inboxCards, {}), status = readPresentation(page.read); return <>{matchRead(page.read, { loading: () => "Loading…", denied: why => why.message,
    absent: () => "No conversations", partial: () => "Partial results", present: rows => `${rows.length} conversations` })}
    {status.incomplete && "Results may be incomplete"}{page.items.map(row => <p key={row.id}>{row.title} ({row.memberCount})</p>)}<button disabled={!page.hasMore} onClick={page.loadMore}>More</button></>;
}
```

- `E_OFFLINE`: unavailable; reconnect.
- `E_QUERY_RETRY`: exhausted; retry.

## Reference

`Write`: pending custody, fresh sent result, replay without result, or failed. Clear on sentResult; undefined is not success.
```tsx
import { useState } from "react"; import { matchRead, sentResult, useAppReady, useMutation, useQuery } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function Composer({conversationId}:{conversationId:string}) {
  const [draft,setDraft]=useState(""),[submitted,setSubmitted]=useState("");
  const ready=useAppReady(),send=useMutation(api.send),member=useQuery(api.myMembership,{conversationId});
  const blocked=matchRead(member,{denied:()=>true,absent:read=>read.fresh&&!read.revalidating,partial:()=>false,otherwise:()=>false});
  async function submit(){const text=draft.trim();if(!ready||blocked||!text||text.length>2000)return;setSubmitted(text);const write=await send.run({conversationId,body:text});if(sentResult(write)!==undefined)setDraft(current=>current.trim()===text?"":current);}
  return <><input value={draft} onChange={e=>setDraft(e.target.value)}/>
    <button disabled={!ready||send.inFlight||blocked} onClick={()=>void submit()}>Send</button>
    {send.last?.state==="failed"&&submitted&&<p role="alert">Not sent: {submitted} <button onClick={()=>setDraft(current=>current?current+" "+submitted:submitted)}>Edit</button> <button onClick={()=>setSubmitted("")}>Dismiss</button></p>}</>;
}
```
Membership: no horizon, the conversation's own group and messages' audience prove presence/absence unless a pending prediction blocks absence. `loading` means acquisition or later withdrawal. `existence(member) === "absent"` blocks only when fresh and non-revalidating; denied blocks; loading/partial never disable. Never add `with own keys` to admin-written memberships.
Draft/receipt owners, password/list props: [reference](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md). `waitSeconds:0` returns custody; keep the original id, never auto-resend.

## An online role form across releases
Create createApp({url,writes:"online",sessionStore:localStorageSessionStore()}) once outside render; mount under AppProvider.
```tsx
import { useEffect, useState } from "react"; import { isPresent, matchReadList, sentResult, useAppReady, useMutation, useQuery, useRelease } from "snapback4/react"; import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
export function StaffWorkspace({team}: {team: string}) {
  const [draft, setDraft] = useState(""), [selected, setSelected] = useState("");
  const ready = useAppReady(), release = useRelease(), role = useQuery(api.myRole, {team});
  const [lastStaff, setLastStaff] = useState(false);
  const staff = isPresent(role) && role.data.role === "staff", uncertain = "loading" in role || ("data" in role && role.existence === "partial");
  useEffect(() => { if (!uncertain) setLastStaff(staff); }, [uncertain, staff]);
  const shell = uncertain ? lastStaff : staff;
  const available = api.addStaffNote.available(release) && api.staffNotes.available(release), add = useMutation(api.addStaffNote);
  async function submit() {const title = draft.trim(); if (!ready || !staff || !available || !title || !selected) return;
    const write = await add.run({team, title, category:selected}); if (sentResult(write) !== undefined) setDraft(current => current === draft ? "" : current);}
  return <section aria-label="Staff workspace"><p>{shell ? "Staff workspace" : "Staff access required"}</p>
    <label>Note<input value={draft} onChange={e => setDraft(e.target.value)} /></label>
    <label htmlFor="category">Category</label><select id="category" value={selected} onChange={e => setSelected(e.target.value)}><option value="">Choose</option><option value="general">General</option></select>
    <button disabled={!ready || !staff || !available || add.inFlight} onClick={() => void submit()}>Save note</button>
    {staff && available ? <StaffRows team={team} /> : <p>{"denied" in role || "denied" in release || ("generation" in release && !available) || (!uncertain && !staff) ? "Staff workspace not available" : "Waiting for staff capabilities"}</p>}
    {add.last?.state === "failed" && <p role="alert">{add.last.why.message}</p>}
  </section>;
}
function StaffRows({team}: {team:string}) {
  const rows = useQuery(api.staffNotes, {team}); return <>{matchReadList(rows, {denied: why => <p role="alert">{why.message}</p>, absent: () => <p>No notes</p>,
    present: notes => <ul>{notes.map(row => <li key={row.id}>{row.title} — {row.priority ?? "normal"}</li>)}</ul>,
    otherwise: () => <p>Updating notes…</p>})}</>;
}
```
Mount `<StaffWorkspace key={team} team={team}/>`: drafts survive loading/releases; resource/account/backend changes reset them. Sticky role labels only the shell. Current role/availability gate reads/Save; rules decide permission. "Since release N" fields stay optional: `row.priority ?? "normal"` at React/Exact boundaries.

## A public guest page

Guests (`use identity`/`use identity guests`) access public reads. Browser session custody is required; no device/password.
```tsx
import { createApp } from "snapback4/app";
import { localStorageSessionStore } from "snapback4/auth";
import { AppProvider, matchReadList, useAuth, useQuery } from "snapback4/react";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api";
const guestApp = createApp({url:"http://127.0.0.1:4400", readOnly:true, sessionStore:localStorageSessionStore()});
export function GuestPage() { return <AppProvider app={guestApp}><GuestGate /></AppProvider>; }
function GuestGate() {
  const auth = useAuth();
  if (auth.state === "restoring") return <p>Restoring…</p>;
  if (auth.state === "signed-out") return <><button onClick={() => void auth.guest()}>Continue as a guest</button><p role="alert">{auth.why?.message}</p></>;
  return <PublicNotes />;
}
function PublicNotes() {
  const notes = useQuery(api.publicNotes, {});
  return <>{matchReadList(notes, {denied: why => <p role="alert">{why.message}</p>, absent: () => <p>No public notes</p>,
    present: rows => <ul>{rows.map(row => <li key={row.id}>{row.title}</li>)}</ul>, otherwise: () => <p>Loading…</p>})}</>;
}
```

## React hooks and auth decisions

Render sign-in controls while signed-out, Restoring… otherwise. AppProvider resets on app/viewer changes. `useAppReady()` gates writers/proof input after app.ready and signed-in auth without admission refusal; later sign-in recovers. Readiness is not permission/connectivity; surface auth.why.
`writes:"online"` with device/readOnly yields signed-out `useAuth().why` and failed app.ready. Recreate corrected. Missing browser storage still throws `E_ADMISSION`.
`useAuth()` members: `state`, `viewer`, `why`, `signup(email, password)`, `login(email, password)`, `guest()`, `logout()`, `logoutEverywhere()`. `signOut()` belongs to the client. Expo session store: secureStoreSessionStore(SecureStore); password form: [reference](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md#password-react-and-expo).

| Helper | Input/result | Handlers/arguments |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `matchRead(read, handlers)` | Read or whole page; handler result | `loading(read)`, `denied(why,read)`, `absent(read)`, `partial(data,read)`, `present(data,read)`. Bare `why: Refusal`; data first, metadata second. All five required, even partial on single rows; `otherwise(read)` replaces omitted loading/absent/present only. |
| `matchReadList(read, handlers)` | List read or page | Required `denied(why,read)`, `otherwise(read)`; other handlers above optional. Omitted partial uses otherwise. |
| `otherwiseAll(fallback, overrides?)` | Spread into matchRead handlers | `fallback(read)` includes denied/partial; named handlers win in any order. |
| `usePage(op, args)` | Query with `c: cursor ?`; omit c from args | `{read, items, hasMore, loadMore}`; match page.read/page, render items, call loadMore(). No handlers. |

`'data' in read` exposes data/existence/metadata; data may be null, partial or stale. Denied: “not available”. Complete empty lists are absent; `absent` can be an authorised empty list. `isPresent(read)` excludes null; `isReady(read)` permits null; `existence(read)` and `read.existence` classify.
```ts
import type { Read } from "snapback4/contract";
export function listLabel(read: Read<readonly {id:string}[]>): string {
  if ('denied' in read) return "not available"; if ('data' in read) return read.existence === "absent" ? "No rows" : `${read.data.length} rows`; return "Loading…"; }
```
`loading`: keep shells; `denied`: show refusal; `present`: render; `absent`: proven empty; `partial`: uncertain. Offline absent is absence at the acquired watermark. Hidden/missing rows are indistinguishable. `readPresentation` notices never grant permission. Render page.items, follow hasMore/loadMore. Unknown counts never hide conversations.
Generated `predicts` controls timing: eligible/device/held facts may render `pending:true` before `useMutation().run()` settles; otherwise queues without a row. Never/online: no prediction; data follows server settlement/delivery. run() waits for sent/failed; `{waitSeconds:0}` returns custody, possibly pending. Proofs await fresh sent; predictions aren't settlement.
`query(op,args)` settles ready/denied within 30 seconds. Unavailable offline reads give immediate `E_OFFLINE`. After four rounds, exhaustion refuses retryable `E_QUERY_RETRY`; permanently unavailable held reads refuse non-retryable `E_QUERY_HELD`. Retention, RowOf/ArgsOf/ResultOf and {skip}: [reference](CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md). Share/paginate: eight active, eight queued observation attempts; direct queries and timed presence refreshes use separate paths.
Browser createApp: no sync → journal; sync → webDevice(); online writes: writes:"online". Native: nativeDevice; Expo Go online. Expo: `npx snapback4 init --expo`, `npm install`, `npx snapback4 check`. Peers: Expo >=57.0.22 <58, core >=57.0.23 <58; RN 0.86.3, React/React DOM 19.2.3. `npx snapback4@latest upgrade` aligns SDK packages. Regenerate iOS/rebuild after native changes; every milestone: npm run export:ios; proof in the background: witness status --wait. Rebuild: npm run native:smoke. Acquisition/offline reopen/reconnect/write replay: guide witness.
useOnline: server reads/ephemeral/actions; durable writes: app.mutate. SDK owns delivery reporting; screen owns visible `seen` reporting; useSeen retries receipts (guide receipts). Read-only: guide read-only. Media: OFFLINE-UPLOADS.md. Proofs: await fresh sent, then rendering; avoid networkidle (guide testing).
Keyed counts: hidden key → null; authorized missing count group → 0; public count → number. Choose count ?? 0 explicitly.
import { releaseLoading, type ReleaseSnapshot } from "./release.ts";
export type { ReleaseSnapshot, ServedRelease } from "./release.ts";
import { hasUploads } from "./uploads.ts";
import { mintId } from "./client.ts";
import { settleMutation } from "./write-settlement.ts";
import { Following } from "./following.ts";
import type { FollowingQuery } from "./contract.ts";
export type { FollowingPhase, FollowingSnapshot } from "./following.ts";
import { readOnlyRefusal } from "./read-only.ts";
import { pendingItems, pendingState, observeIntents, intentReadSequence, type PendingItemsOptions, type PendingStateOptions, type PendingIntent, type MutationArgs } from "./pending-items.ts";
import { pendingItemsSource } from "./react-pending-items.ts";
export type { PendingItem, PendingIntent, IntentPhase, PendingItemsOptions, PendingStateOptions } from "./pending-items.ts";
import { inspect, type ReadInspection } from "./inspection.ts";
import { assetError, assetRetryAt } from "./asset-download.ts";
import { saveAsset, type Saved } from "./files.ts";
import { assetWaiting, assetWaitDelay, readAsset, type AssetProgressDetail, type AssetStage, type AssetWaiting } from "./asset-progress.ts";
export type { AssetStage, AssetWaiting } from "./asset-progress.ts";
// The React arm: one provider and hooks over the client card.
// Read hooks return the client card vocabulary; inspection is an opt-in diagnostic.

import { createContext, useCallback, useContext, useEffect, useMemo, useRef, useState, useSyncExternalStore, type ReactNode } from "react";
import type { ObserveOptions, Act, Action, ActOptions, AssetRead, Client, Cursor, Link, Ephemeral, Signal, Mutation, MutateOptions, Observed, PendingWrite, Principal, Query, Read, Refusal, ResultOf, Write } from "./contract.ts";
import type { Session } from "./auth.ts";
import type { App, AppAuth, AuthSnapshot, Online } from "./app.ts";
import { acquisition, type Acquisition } from "./acquisition.ts";
export type { Acquisition } from "./acquisition.ts";
import { clockDelay, onClock } from "./clock.ts";
import { isReady } from "./contract.ts";
export { matchRead, matchReadList, type ReadMatchListHandlers, otherwiseAll, isReady, isPresent, isLoading, isDenied, isFailed, sentResult, existence, readPresentation, type ReadMatchHandlers, type ReadMatchFallbackHandlers, type ReadMatchOtherwiseAll, type ReadMatchOverrides, type OtherwiseAll, type ReadPresentation } from "./contract.ts";
export type { Client, Principal, Read, Ready, SettledRead, Write, Signal, Act, Outcome, Refusal, Query, Mutation, Ephemeral, Action, MutateOptions, PendingWrite, Link, Cursor, ArgsOf, ResultOf, RowOf } from "./contract.ts";
// React apps name the app they hand to <AppProvider> here; `createApp` itself
// stays in snapback4/app so this entry never imports the session runtime.
export type { App, AppOptions, AppAuth, AuthSnapshot, AuthState, AppResult, Online } from "./app.ts";
import { Pages } from "./react-pages.ts";
import { pendingWritesSource } from "./react-pending-writes.ts";
import { peekObservation } from "./observe-watch.ts";
import { readKey } from "./read-key.ts";

import { SeenOwner, type SeenOptions, type SeenSnapshot } from "./seen-progress.ts";
import type { ReceiptFamily } from "./receipt-family.ts";
export type { SeenOptions, SeenSnapshot } from "./seen-progress.ts";

const ClientContext = createContext<Client | null>(null);

const AppContext = createContext<App | null>(null);

/** Own the app outside render; changing accounts replaces the client context. */
export function AppProvider({ app, children }: { app: App; children: ReactNode }) {
  const snapshot = useSyncExternalStore(app.auth.onChange, app.auth.getSnapshot, app.auth.getSnapshot);
  const [boundary, setBoundary] = useState(() => ({ app, generation: 0 }));
  // Account identity is state, never a memo cache that React may discard.
  if (boundary.app !== app) setBoundary({ app, generation: boundary.generation + 1 });
  return <AppContext.Provider value={app}><ClientContext.Provider value={app.client}>
    <AppAccount key={`${boundary.generation}:${snapshot.viewer ?? ""}`}>{children}</AppAccount>
  </ClientContext.Provider></AppContext.Provider>;
}

// Remount account-owned component state, including drafts and asset handles.
function AppAccount({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) { return children; }

export function useAuth(): AuthSnapshot & Pick<AppAuth, "signup" | "login" | "guest" | "logout" | "logoutEverywhere"> {
  const app = useContext(AppContext);
  if (!app) throw new Error("useAuth: no <AppProvider> above this component");
  const snapshot = useSyncExternalStore(app.auth.onChange, app.auth.getSnapshot, app.auth.getSnapshot);
  return { ...snapshot, signup: app.auth.signup, login: app.auth.login, guest: app.auth.guest,
    logout: app.auth.logout, logoutEverywhere: app.auth.logoutEverywhere };
}

/** Initial opening has finished and the current signed-in account admits writes.
 * This is admission readiness, not network availability or permission. */
export function useAppReady(): boolean {
  const app = useContext(AppContext);
  if (!app) throw new Error("useAppReady: no <AppProvider> above this component");
  const auth = useSyncExternalStore(app.auth.onChange, app.auth.getSnapshot, app.auth.getSnapshot);
  const [opened, setOpened] = useState<App | null>(null);
  useEffect(() => {
    let active = true;
    const finished = () => { if (active) setOpened(app); };
    void app.ready.then(finished, finished);
    return () => { active = false; };
  }, [app]);
  return opened === app && auth.state === "signed-in" && !auth.why?.code.startsWith("E_ADMISSION");
}

export function SnapbackProvider({ client, children }: { client: Client; children: ReactNode }) {
  return <ClientContext.Provider value={client}>{children}</ClientContext.Provider>;
}

export function useClient(): Client {
  const client = useContext(ClientContext);
  if (!client) throw new Error("useClient: no <AppProvider> or <SnapbackProvider> above this component");
  return client;
}

/** Served capabilities follow generation changes, including when query gates are closed. */
export function useRelease(): ReleaseSnapshot {
  const client = useClient();
  const source = client.release;
  const subscribe = useCallback((notify: () => void) => source?.subscribe(notify) ?? (() => {}), [source]);
  const getSnapshot = useCallback(() => source?.getSnapshot() ?? releaseLoading, [source]);
  return useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, getSnapshot, () => releaseLoading);
}

/** The app-owned server arm; follows account changes and credential rotation. */
export function useOnline(): Online {
  const app = useContext(AppContext);
  if (!app) throw new Error("useOnline: no <AppProvider> above this component");
  return useSyncExternalStore(app.onOnlineChange, () => app.online, () => app.online);
}

llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:43:Restoration, candidate validation, derived rebuild, catalog/generation/programs and current release commit together. Any error rolls all of them back. Retained values overwrite no explicit candidate writes: restoration precedes activation builders. Incompatible name reuse must pass candidate types and constraints; no flag bypasses validation.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:57:Release activation events live in the database transaction with activation revision, operation, release/previous/generation, timestamp and retained/restored summary. Prune records its item summary in that transaction. Existing releases.jsonl and current remain checked convenience copies; post-commit filesystem errors explicitly say activation succeeded. History is never edited or rewound. Rollback picks an earlier immutable source, not a historical data snapshot.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:65:snapback4 releases <dir> reads authoritative current release, activation revision, generation and durable events without adopting source, including while serve is live. This is the recovery command for an ambiguous result. A bounded command message identifies the owned database inode/canonical identity, candidate release id, expected current release and monotone activation revision, operation and explicit flags. The server compiles only its own immutable release directory, prepares native module/worker prerequisites before commit, then holds its existing engine writer guard for activation and in-memory publication. The server verifies both expected current release and activation revision inside the activation transaction. Increment activation revision on every successful release activation, including an equal-hash source change; A→B→A cannot satisfy an old command. Concurrent commands serialize on the command flock; out-of-band stale commands fail compare-and-swap. No request may choose arbitrary filesystem source. Compilation/validation failure leaves the previous engine, bound schema, native module, worker revision and reader factory unchanged.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:67:A query already running in a pinned SQLite read transaction finishes on its old snapshot and reports that generation. A writer already executing finishes before activation obtains its engine guard. Queued HTTP operations are admitted under one release request guard covering signature lookup, argument decoding and execution. Activation acquires its exclusive side before taking the engine guard; admitted operations finish before activation, and later operations dispatch under the new generation. Internal detached readers still fence obsolete identities with E_GENERATION. Fresh requests after publication use the new engine; a stale pooled reader not yet pinned retries through the replacement factory. Prepare a runtime bundle containing bound programs, precompiled native module/metadata, worker root and validated named declarations; prepare the reader factory from the post-activation engine while its writer guard blocks new writers (reader opening failure after commit clears the old factory and uses existing lazy retry, never aborts a committed activation or advertises the old backend). Swap bound/generation/native under the common bound guard in the existing /schema guard order, retire pooled readers, rebind ephemeral state, invalidate change answers, wake sync/polls and update worker revision while holding the writer guard. The supervisor explicitly switches its immutable release project root and named declarations on revision, including addition/removal/replacement; externally supplied --worker stays an operator-owned entry. Worker preparation captures an immutable (root, entry, declarations/environment, supervisor revision) tuple. Under the engine guard it compares that captured revision to the current worker revision before minting credentials; stale preparation is discarded and rediscovered. Never relabel an old prepared source with the latest revision. Credentials bind that checked revision so later activation fences them even when spawn finishes later. Workers restart using the candidate packaged source and native continuation hashes keep the existing conservative parking policy. Long validation can pause writes; hot means no process restart, not an unbounded zero-pause latency promise.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:147:Every changed execution activation including A→B→A gets a fresh monotone semantic generation, never reuses a release number as a generation. Execution-content identity includes the prepared native module digest as well as schema/programs. A changed native digest advances the existing semantic generation atomically with release activation, even when native declarations are unchanged; removing a module changes the digest too. Equal execution content retains generation unless a release activation changes the final active/dormant population (§9.1), which requires a new replica generation; source-only activations still advance activation revision. The digest is SHA-256 over the exact prepared wasm bytes; the no-module sentinel is the empty string persisted as release_native_digest. activation_hash binds schema/programs and that digest. Comparison is with the current activation, so A→B→A is fresh. Startup attaches the persisted execution identity and verifies the immutable release module matches its recorded digest. This uses the existing device protocol, not a separate native epoch. Rollback and forward use the same generation replacement and full partition reacquisition already used by adoption: /schema advertises the exact active shape, sync rejects old watermarks, devices clear obsolete base/index state and rebase or refuse their queued intents under the existing authority/operation contract. Store id remains unchanged; this is not a store replacement. No dormant rows or fields enter snapshots, partition logs, online answers or device stores. Never combine old-generation pages with a new backend. A late old read remains tagged old and cannot overwrite a newly observed generation. Simulate rollback/forward across a delayed sync page and audience changes; retained data changes server storage only, not device persistence formats.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:149:Lock order: command flock is CLI-only; serving control never acquires it. Candidate filesystem/native/worker preparation precedes engine lock. Serving publication holds engine → bound → generation → native, and separately replaces readers while engine is held (matching existing checkout); no /schema handler takes engine while holding bound. Ephemeral rebind uses its existing committed infallible path, never a fallible post-commit store read. Update desired worker root before revision; worker thread releases root/bound guards before taking engine. Wake/seq/answers follow existing publish_engine while engine is held: these are short memory updates/nonblocking condition notifications, required before new writer admission; no socket/child wait occurs there. `releases` includes the existing worker status metadata so post-commit launch failure is visible as degraded availability. No SQLite lock is held while waiting for a child process or socket peer.

Also prune preview/confirmation and asset lifetime; unique failure after dormant-index writes; required default-less forward refusal; transaction failure leaves active and dormant data intact; restart binds the committed release; simultaneous hot requests and failed hot activation; stale-generation sync and real client reacquisition; simulator delayed pages/audiences. Quality bar: the commissioned core/lang/device/sim cargo suites, CLI release/serve/adoption tests, relevant client tests and ref-check. No data-loss claim rests on diagnostics alone.
