llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:238:Implementer: REL, 2026-10-12. This supersedes §3's narrowing refusal and §5's exposed request-generation race for release serving. HAND owns device version negotiation; DX owns production-writer diagnostics and starter/password UI.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:242:Each actual release activation, including roll-forward and equal-schema/program-only/native-only selection, runs the row-visibility closure. A visibility change advances generation even if execution hashes are equal, so devices replace their population; identical-source deploy no-ops do not activate. The closure removes authoritative rows whose **present** values do not fit the candidate column type or text constraint (asset leaves include ledger-backed kind, byte bound and file MIME allow-list, recursively through optionals/lists) into `_dormant_narrow_rows(t,id,data,definition,reason)`. Store format advances to `snapback4-release-store-3`; this binary opens format 2 and upgrades only during committed activation. Absent new required fields remain E_ADOPT. Engine metadata and maintained cache fields are not a narrowing trigger. Store the full authoritative image and its source table definition (merge restored whole-table and column definitions before classifying restored values), with interpreted old literal defaults and nulls, before projection. Restore any retained image that fits the candidate, splitting omitted columns into existing dormant cells. Restoration is automatic even on deploy; this is an existing row lifetime, not reuse of a removed schema name. No fallback syntax ships: the declared policy is hidden rows, never value substitution.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:244:Visibility is a fixed point: a row with a non-null reference (including nested/list references) to a dormant narrowed row is also dormant. This covers authorization parents and memberships. Active programs, rules, point reads, lists, counts, caps, maintained views, assets and application SQL see only active r/i. Tentative restore-and-rehide work does not count as a visibility change; compute population change from newly hidden rows and successfully restored rows in the final closed population. All dependent physical indexes and aggregates rebuild when rows enter or leave dormancy, including otherwise unchanged tables. Sync generation replacement clears base/index images and acquisition, then reacquires only that same active population. This supersedes the device literal-default image-retention optimization: an additive default can restore a dormant cell whose actual value is incompatible, so schema shape alone cannot certify an old image. Same-generation attachment and identical-source no-ops preserve images; outbox custody survives generation replacement. HAND owns the shipped-device compatibility floor; DX owns updates to client/evolution guide text. A reference lookup to a dormant id is absent. Preserve LLP 3000.039 §1/§4.4: references validate a nominal id, not target existence. A new write may carry a dormant id just as it may carry an unknown/hidden id; read-through or explicit require/insert rules decide target admission. Such a write remains active under the old release until the next adoption's reference-closure pass, but grants no parent-derived authority. Never probe dormancy selectively at the reference write boundary, which would be an existence oracle. Dormant rows contribute neither to uniqueness nor caps. Restoration uses the same set-null cap exemption as ordinary writes; null-exempt groups are never counted by adoption or successor assessment. Their ids remain reserved: inserts cannot replace them and return the same E_RULE as a hidden live identity conflict, without row contents or a special dormant code. Updates/deletes by old programs see an absent row; active updates retain unrelated dormant cells. Dormant uniqueness keys are not reserved: old writes may cause a constraint conflict on forward, which E_ADOPT reports with executable application SQL; resolve that conflict under the current release when those rows are active. If both conflict rows are dormant, deploy a bridge with compatible widened types and the conflicting index non-unique/cap increased using --restore-dormant, restore both, repair with authorized mutations, then reinstate the constraint. Neither row silently wins; activation refuses atomically. Diagnostics include this bridge and application SQL, with values only in privileged operator output (§3). Type incompatibility alone never blocks switching.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:250:`rollback <dir> --to N --check` and `check --store <path> --release N` execute candidate adoption on a private SQLite snapshot created by VACUUM INTO through a read-only source connection. This is disk-backed, uses the exact adoption builder/validation, and never changes the live source, pointer, events or generation. SQLite supports VACUUM INTO on a read-only source; it acquires a coherent WAL read snapshot without the source writer lock. BUSY/disk failures refuse with retry advice, never fall back to copying sidecars. The temporary directory is owner-only and removed on success, refusal and ordinary unwinding. Process kill/crash may leave a private preview directory; operators can remove snapback4-release-preview-* leftovers when no preview uses them. The live format, including format 2, is never upgraded by preview. The snapshot may require disk/time proportional to the store; it provides a point-in-time plan, not a write freeze. The output enumerates the full candidate inventory and totals every narrowed population, not just the first sixteen status items; it includes any E_ADOPT refusal. Preview prepares the candidate native content identity but never starts workers or effects.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:252:Deploy warns when type widening (including image/video byte bounds and file MIME/byte sets) admits values the previous release refuses; rollback also warns when its selected target admits values the current release refuses. All E_ADOPT inspect SQL uses quoted application table identifiers accepted by `sql`, including Quarry names that are SQL keywords; internal dormant inventory is inspected through `releases`. `latest` denotes the highest complete immutable source release, independently of current release and monotone generation. Complete here means a numeric directory with nonempty source that compiles; empty permanently reserved crash directories are skipped. Native preparation still validates a selected release before activation. Packaging and comparing the content digest precedes allocating a numeric release. Identical packaged source is a no-op: no new release directory, activation revision, audit or generation. A no-op is not an activation and therefore does not advance §5's activation revision. Widening warnings go to stderr, are advisory, and do not change a successful exit status. `releases <dir>` adds a JSON latest field; `releases <dir> [N|latest]` adds selected source id/digest/currentness without changing the authoritative current identity or activating; `rollback --to latest` and preview `--release latest` accept it. Command completion follows in-memory publication and prints `release N is current as generation G`.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:256:Integration 6: the generation footer shares the 16 MiB response budget; footer overflow replaces the partial payload with the ordinary bounded E_BOUND envelope. Unauthenticated 401 responses disclose no generation. The generation header is exposed through CORS on JSON, native and asset responses.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:258:Buffer bounded request bytes before final release admission. A fair release request lease then spans each one-shot /q and /m's release-dependent lookup, typed argument decoding, execution and reply-identity capture. Release it before serialization/socket output, including every refusal/replay path. A scoped cleanup releases it on unwinding/early exits. Queued exclusive activation prevents new shared admissions, avoiding writer starvation. Release activation takes the exclusive lease across adoption and publication. Acquire it before engine/bound/readers locks. Sync and held polls retain their existing generation negotiation and wake behavior, so a long poll cannot block release publication. The guard admits concurrent readers; activation may pause new operations while it waits for current ones. Dev retains predecessor snapshot reading during adoption. Each engine JSON reply carries `generation`; custom application route bodies remain byte-owned by the application and carry the header. query, schema and sync envelopes that already specify their pinned generation retain that value; replay and generic refusals use the admitted request generation. Authentication, asset preparation/admission (including early refusals), durable actions/effects, service minting and ephemeral work share an engine-work accessor that stamps under the actual producer lock after buffered intake. Authentication enablement is rechecked against that same engine schema at credential collection/mint, including after KDF; successful ancillary credential checks and commit notifications do not relabel a captured result. Bound-only/raw metadata captures its own bundle identity. Durable actions stamp the engine that admits or collects after buffered intake, without holding a release lease during worker waits. Raw native/asset/following/application-route responses use the header, without modifying owned bytes. Stamp it while holding the engine that authorizes/collects the result, after body intake; a request-arrival stamp alone cannot label a later engine generation. The HTTP X-Snapback-Generation header carries the same value. Stream/asset replies use the header. Unknown operations return 404 denied E_OP; typed mutation argument decoding and range failures return 200 failed E_INPUT (with the request id when supplied); query argument failures and malformed JSON retain their 400 denied input envelope. No one-shot release operation exposes adoption loading images or E_GENERATION.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:268:Round two: Muse READY; GPT NOT READY on four implementation gaps. Fixed and tested: prune/inventory asset homes now match retained row identities, equal-schema activations rerun closure and force a generation plus cache rebuild on population change, typed mutation decoding uses the promised failed envelope, and previews enumerate all inventory items. The GPT example alleging enum string rejection in wire::from_json was not accurate (the function converts text, and the engine validates the enum), but the broader wrong-shape/extra-argument envelope finding was valid and was fixed; the stress test now asserts the old-release enum failure unconditionally. Added focused tests for partial restoration/prune, legacy format-2 deletion evidence, equal-schema nominal references, mutation decoding and large inventories. Round three reviews the clarified amendment and these deltas independently.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:271:Round three: Muse READY; GPT NOT READY on two additional restoration cases. Fixed: restored whole-table/column images now participate in closure even when absent from the immediate predecessor, using their retained source definitions; a tentative restore immediately hidden again does not advance generation. Regressions cover restore into a narrower table/column followed by another retirement/forward cycle, and equal-content reselection of a hidden parent/child with no visibility change. Round four independently checks the final amended rules and implementation.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:274:Round four: Muse READY; GPT NOT READY on three combined cases. Fixed and tested: retained lifetime evidence survives transfer into narrowed storage (whole tables and cells, including deleted/reused parents), partial restorations remain explicit in paged audit items, and following/route responses stamp the executing/collecting engine generation after intake. Maintained cache fields are excluded from narrowing classification, as §9.1 states. A deterministic following request/intake switch regression joins the HTTP load test. Round five independently checks these corrections and the final revision.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:277:Round five: Muse READY; GPT NOT READY on an audit decrease caused by expiry being mislabeled as restoration. Fixed with explicit committed column-consumption counters and an expiry regression. An additional device check found that the former literal-default image-retention optimization could present a now-dormant restored value under its fallback default. Generation replacement now always clears base/acquisition before reacquiring, on native and wasm, with a retired-column/literal-default regression on both stores; same-generation attachments and durable intents remain intact. Round six reviews these final corrections independently.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:281:Round seven: Muse READY; GPT found expired narrowed images losing source deadlines when read-through is removed, initially dangling keep references misclassified during original whole-table capture, and action replies using intake generation. Fixed source-image retirement before projection, unbound classification at the original capture boundary, and action admission/collection stamps; regression coverage added. Audit counts and adoption receipts now use final surviving restoration identities after every activation phase.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:287:Round eleven: Muse READY; GPT found auth/guest minting under a new engine with the intake generation label. Producer capture is now shared across authentication and remaining worker/metadata paths, with intake-switch regressions. Explicit releases N/latest inspection fulfills the literal selector request in addition to the latest listing field.

 succeeded in 0ms:
 * `cut` names a disclosed meter or word-limit cut. Non-uniform server reads
 * withhold it: null means "not disclosed here", not "not cut".
 * An empty meter-cut page is partial: retry narrower, or continue if `next` exists.
 * `fresh` is transport truth: the value reflects the server now, as far as the
 * change feed can tell. When it does not, `since` is the wall-clock time (ms) of
 * the newest server state it does reflect, so a screen can say "as of three minutes ago".
 * Observations discard responses older than observed commits in the same store and
 * permanently refuse on store replacement or regression.
 * `connected[p]` is the exception. No commit announces that `connected[p]` changed, so an answer of a query that
 * may read it is a sample: it keeps the `fresh` it arrived with (true for a server's whole answer, false when
 * a local answer composes retained rows) until a newer answer replaces it. A background re-read about
 * 5 seconds after each reply settles does that while refreshes succeed; failed refreshes or a suspended
 * client leave the sample on screen, with the same flag, and no bound on its age.
 * A mounted same-key observation retains its answer during plain revalidation,
 * marked `revalidating: true`, until replacement, with no timeout. Transport
 * failure marks `fresh: false`; retry exhaustion also keeps the answer. Local
 * commits, durable/ephemeral change evidence and /sync publications start one
 * non-renewable second at the replacement attempt; capacity queue wait pauses
 * this budget. Online change
 * evidence can be revocation, so its second starts on receipt and includes queue
 * wait. Failure or timeout withdraws; a later accepted answer can still publish.
 * Applied local scope loss withdraws dependent audience groups immediately,
 * including staged removals; older replica answers wait for publication.
 * `keep: false` withdraws immediately; `keep: true` adds a 30 s subscriber-free
 * linger. Gate actions on `isReady(read) && !read.revalidating` when they
 * require a completed re-read.
 * Every option withdraws on expiry, a refusal, a viewer or credential change,
 * store reset, adoption or close. Retention never grants authority.
 * @ref LLP 3000.023#10 — all change evidence bounds retention.
 * Before the first answer use `loading`; a refusal carries its message in `denied`.
 */
// @ref LLP 3000.031#1.1 — existence describes the disclosed value, never hidden rows.
export interface ReadyMetadata {
  readonly complete: boolean;
  readonly cut: ReadCut;
  readonly fresh: boolean;
  readonly revalidating?: true;
  readonly since?: number;
  readonly next?: Cursor | null;
  readonly meter?: ReadMeter;
}
// @ref LLP 3000.031#3 — named arms preserve generic covariance.
// Every arm retains T; absent additionally proves null or an empty array.
interface Present<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {
  readonly existence: "present";
  readonly data: T & ({} | undefined);
}
interface Partial<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {
- an incarnation change still resets. `keep: false` is the choice for a screen whose actions must
never see a revoked row, such as an export, a copy, or playback gated on
`isReady(read)`.

### 3.3 `revalidating`, not `fresh`

`fresh: false` already means "not confirmed by the server as current". It
describes a completed offline or local answer (`local-query.ts:303`), and
waiting for `fresh: true` can block a valid offline workflow indefinitely. A
retained answer during a re-read gets its own flag instead:

```ts
{ data, complete, cut, fresh, since?, next?, revalidating?: true }
```

An application that acts on an answer can wait for `!read.revalidating`,
which a successful re-read clears within the bound.

### 3.4 Hard withdrawals stay exact

Retention never crosses these; each still withdraws at once, for every watch.
Their mechanism is `reset` or a `denied` publish (`read-observation.ts:77–84`,
`:96`), which must never start retaining, and a fetch with `retain: false`
(the deadline retry, `local-query.ts:185–187`, `:278–281`), which still
withdraws:

- **Expiry.** An answer's `expire at` deadline withdraws it at that time,
  with a re-read in flight, through clock corrections and the two-phase sweep
  (`read-expiry.ts:16`, `:37`; `local.ts:942`; `client.ts:599–601`).
  - Remembered answers and late bodies keep their expiry barriers across a
    clock rollback (`local-reads.ts:72`, `:102`; `local-query.ts:264`).
  - As today, a suspended page runs no timer (`CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md:829`).
- **Who is looking, and what the store is.** Each withdraws at once:
  - a credential rotation or a live viewer change;
  - sign-out, which publishes `denied` (`client.ts:318`, `local.ts:354`);
  - store replacement or restore (`client.ts:356`, `local.ts:256–270`);
  - `adopt`, a store reset, a generation change or the first schema
    installation, each a `reset({ loading: true })` for every watch
    (`local.ts:330`, `client.ts:221–223`, `:742–747`);
  - an ephemeral incarnation change (`invalidate(true)`,
    `ephemeral-reads.ts:62–70`);
  - `clear_rows` and `settle_exit`, the `reset` branch of `invalidateAnswers`
    (`local-reads.ts:121`), which must not be caught up in the ordinary
scope-removal check and delivery approval still apply. A scope tombstone
withdraws immediately even when it is already published. Covered replies do
not lower the replica floor, change the eight-attempt queue or renew retention
budgets. Memory's cut/ticket ordering and expiry barriers remain independent;
this proof does not renew an old cache ticket.

The deterministic process and wasm regression publishes the first matching
reply while forcing sync before every response; the prior client discards six
successive matching replies. The OP report records the three-browser stage
traces, measurements and stressed routing/retention controls.

Only a `held` watch parks before evaluation until that pending cut applies.
Every watch awaiting the cut rejoins read work at publication even if sync's
eventual footprint is disjoint: an omitted notification
table list conservatively marked every watch. Other attempts rejected by the
cut remain dirty and retry with backoff from 25 ms to the existing one-second
cap, outside active slots. See **Live propagation** below.

A generation mismatch reported by `/changes` is a hard boundary before any
filtering by touched tables, even when the sequence is unchanged and `tables`
is empty. It enters the existing incarnation reset: every displayed and
remembered answer is retired, in-flight evaluations are fenced, and the old
replica cannot answer until backend adoption completes. A failed sync or
backend read keeps that withdrawal; a sequence floor cannot stand in for the
program boundary. On both clients, a notification with an omitted table list
has unknown dependencies and bounds every watch. An advancing empty list is
also unknown; a quiet empty poll preserves authority and completed memory. Quiet presence publication also checks any pending durable
cut, so an older sample cannot settle the episode or restore withdrawn rows.

Ordinary search and ephemeral recovery carry transport failure through final
candidate selection. Entering ephemeral recovery while already offline is a
transport failure unless a held read or prediction separately blocks it;
explicit unavailable results and denials still withdraw. Ephemeral row expiry,
incarnation resets and notification-triggered bounds remain unchanged.
successful sign-in/opening; a replacement app or account starts a new readiness
lifetime. Signed-out/restoring auth remains false. AppProvider's existing
app/viewer boundary still resets account-owned drafts; no stale identity is
retained to conceal revocation or store replacement.

The compiled role shell owns drafts and selection above every readiness,
role and release branch. Sticky role is a label only. Protected reads and
submission require a present, complete, fresh, non-revalidating current role
and generated operation availability, as well as initial readiness. Loading,
partial, denied, absent, stale or revalidating role evidence never licenses
protected children or a submission. SDK/server admission still handles races.
A release switch withdraws protected children while preserving the shell and
its inputs. Explicit revocation withdraws authority immediately.

## 3. Custom production plans

Preview stays read-only; apply writes only the exact compiled schema shown.
Retain bare identity insertion only when identity is absent. Bare identity
allows passwords and guests; identity guests is guests-only; password removes
guests. A guest session is a session: signup/login can replace it through
app.auth, with the ordinary account boundary; it is not a dev persona.

Keep existing blocked-roster bootstrap mutations for compatibility. Extend
planning to a table with an enum column named role and principal identity in
a unique key when no mutation/job inserts or upserts that table, even if the
advisory's approximate reachability misses it. Generate one service:ops-guarded
insert operation per declared role, with that role fixed as a literal, rather
than a caller-supplied role argument. Inputs retain all other writable required
columns and explicit principal defaults. Such provisioning is independent of
fixture values; all declared roles appear even when the current fixture uses
only one. Existing writer paths remain application policy; the planner does
not claim their role coverage. Non-roster and inherited-authority roots stay
explicit review messages. Add only the displayed independent service read and
insert branches; never bypass constraints or rewrite an application's screens.
Fresh names avoid collisions; repeated apply is idempotent once writers exist.
The whole plan must compile before any file write and retain full-source
preflight/symlink refusal. A failed proposed policy writes nothing.

The principal scan excludes every dot-directory, test directories and known
acceptance/fixture/proof/test scripts. Runtime source files under scripts still
get scanned. Findings remain bounded review hints, never a proof of complete
migration. Detect usePersonaApp, persona: and ?as= custom entry sites and point
them at guide production's account/persona recipe; do not rewrite arbitrary UI.
Upgrade names init --adapt --production for a persona-only project.

## 4. Declaration and recipe corrections

Post-baseline row and projected fields explain their optionality with
“optional: releases before N lack this column”; N is the existing configured
since_release label, not an inferred server generation. Public argument labels
retain their existing meaning. A literal string arm of a conditional joins as
its singleton enum domain; enum arms retain their union, including nullable
arms. Dynamic text still widens to text; evaluation/laziness and wire values
are unchanged. This is a precision correction, not a new syntax or IR feature.

Device-less identity createApp calls without sessionStore must produce a type
error naming sessionStore. Device/persona/readOnly forms remain valid. Complete
empty lists already classify as absent; pin that behavior in packed tests.
The client PDF recipe explicitly calls upload with {file:{...}} and executes
against the packed SDK. Other lanes own the literal wire and proof API pages
(KIT4), ordinary starter advice (DAY5), rules recipes (RULES5), and compiler
notes (LANG4).

## 5. Evidence required

H9: execute the packed AccountEntry recipe with a real persisted password
session and held data in a new browser realm, refusing all backend requests,
including auth/config, with no metadata cache. Require signed-in held UI and
no alert. Run packed production preview on hidden checkpoint/scratch and
acceptance/test files alongside a real runtime principal; only runtime findings
appear. Run preview/apply/check/idempotence on copies of three wave-15 custom

READY — no material semantic concerns found.

Reviewed against starter §14, client §§6.10–6.11, release-client §§2–3, and LLP 3000.023’s retention and hard-withdrawal rules.

- **Readiness:** The proposed lifetime consistently separates successful initial opening from continuing admission. Signed-out/restoring states remain false, account/app replacement resets ownership, and later admission warnings cannot erase drafts merely by changing readiness.
- **Authorization:** Sticky role labels grant no authority. Protected children and submission require current, complete, fresh, non-revalidating role evidence plus operation availability. Existing withdrawal rules and server admission cover invalidation and dispatch races.
- **Production adaptation:** Explicitly displayed service grants, literal role provisioning, retained constraints, whole-program compilation and source preflight form a coherent boundary. Preserving existing bootstrap mutations avoids silently breaking callers; existing writer paths explicitly remain application policy.
- **Compatibility:** `since_release` remains an authored label. Conditional-domain refinement preserves nullable alternatives and dynamic-text widening without proposing evaluation or wire changes. The sessionStore requirement preserves the stated device/persona/readOnly exceptions.
- **Testability:** §5 provides concrete acceptance scenarios for offline restoration, release withdrawal/restoration, stale/revoked roles, input identity, readiness recovery, planner idempotence and outsider refusal.

This verdict concerns the proposed semantics. It does not assert implementation completion or passing qualification evidence.
